Is Shai-Hulud Back? Compromised Bitwarden CLI Contains a Self-Propagating npm Worm

Aikido
2026-04-23 11 use cases 5 techniques 6 kill-chain phases detected

Malware found in @bitwarden/cli v2026.4.0 steals SSH keys, cloud secrets, and AI coding tool credentials, then spreads through victims' own npm packages. Inside: a worm calling itself "Shai-Hulud: The Third Coming." Category: Vulnerabilities & Threats

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad). → Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Fake CAPTCHA / clipboard-injected PowerShell (ClickFix / FakeCaptcha) · PowerShell encoded / obfuscated command · Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — Is Shai-Hulud Back? Compromised Bitwarden CLI Contains a Self-Propagating npm Wo
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → Suspicious browser extension installation · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web Network_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("") or RemoteUrl has_any ("audit.checkmarx.cx")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic([""]);
let BadDomains = dynamic(["audit.checkmarx.cx"]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

title: Outbound Connection to Article-Named IP / Domain
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0f
status: experimental
description: |
    Replace `__IP_LIST__` / `__DOMAIN_LIST__` placeholders with
    article-specific destinations. For dynamic IOC management, use a
    watchlist or external feed.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/tactics/TA0011/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.command-and-control
logsource:
    category: network_connection
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_ip:
        DestinationIp:
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__IP_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    selection_domain:
        DestinationHostname:
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_1__'
            - '__DOMAIN_PLACEHOLDER_2__'
    condition: selection_ip or selection_domain
falsepositives:
    - None on a true-positive IOC match
level: critical

title: Browser Extension Installation via Registry
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d0a
status: experimental
description: |
    Registry writes under the browser extension policy keys —
    administrative install of an extension. Often used by adware /
    info-stealers to bypass user-level extension controls.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1176/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1176
    - attack.persistence
logsource:
    category: registry_event
    product: windows
detection:
    selection:
        TargetObject|contains:
            - '\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\'
            - '\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\'
    condition: selection
falsepositives:
    - Group-policy-managed extension whitelists
level: medium
ENDPOINT

    where (Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Registry
    where (Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Google\Chrome\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Microsoft\Edge\Extensions\*"
        OR Registry.registry_path="*\Software\Mozilla\Firefox\Extensions\*")
    by Registry.dest, Registry.registry_path, Registry.registry_value_data, Registry.registry_value_name, Registry.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Registry)`
Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs Actions on Objectives High
Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies SQLite from Chrome/Edge/Firefox.
ATT&CKT1539 T1555.003
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem DeviceFileEvents
DeviceFileEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| where FolderPath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\", @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\", @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where FileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, FolderPath, FileName, ActionType

// ASIM ImFileEvent — covers MDE, Sysmon, AuditD, Defender for Cloud, etc.
ImFileEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| where TargetFilePath has_any (@"\Google\Chrome\User Data\",
                                  @"\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\",
                                  @"\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\")
| where TargetFileName in~ ("Login Data","Cookies","logins.json","cookies.sqlite")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Infostealer - Non-Browser Process Accessing Browser Credential Stores
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d01
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers (RedLine, Lumma, Vidar, Atomic) read Login Data / cookies
    SQLite databases from Chrome/Edge/Firefox profile directories. Any
    non-browser process touching these files is a high-fidelity signal.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1539/
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1555/003/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1539
    - attack.t1555.003
    - attack.credential-access
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Google\Chrome\User Data\'
            - '\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\'
            - '\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\'
    selection_file:
        TargetFilename|endswith:
            - '\Login Data'
            - '\Cookies'
            - '\logins.json'
            - '\cookies.sqlite'
    filter_browser:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\chrome.exe'
            - '\msedge.exe'
            - '\firefox.exe'
            - '\brave.exe'
            - '\opera.exe'
    condition: selection_path and selection_file and not filter_browser
falsepositives:
    - Backup utilities reading user profile files
    - Browser sync agents from non-stock vendors
level: high
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Google\Chrome\User Data\*\Cookies*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Microsoft\Edge\User Data\*\Login Data*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\logins.json*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Mozilla\Firefox\Profiles\*\cookies.sqlite*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","opera.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`

| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")

                                  @"\Atomic\Local Storage\")
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe",
                                  "electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName,
          TargetFilePath, TargetFileName, EventType

title: Crypto-Wallet Keystore Access by Non-Wallet Process
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d02
status: experimental
description: |
    Stealers / wallet drainers read keystore files from MetaMask,
    Exodus, Atomic, Phantom, Bitcoin, Electrum, Ethereum-cli wallets.
    Filtering out the legitimate wallet binaries leaves only attacker
    activity.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1005/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1005
    - attack.collection
logsource:
    category: file_access
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_path:
        TargetFilename|contains:
            - '\Ethereum\keystore\'
            - '\Bitcoin\'
            - '\Exodus\'
            - '\Electrum\wallets\'
            - '\MetaMask\'
            - '\Phantom\'
            - '\Atomic\Local Storage\'
    filter_legit:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\MetaMask.exe'
            - '\Exodus.exe'
            - '\Atomic.exe'
            - '\electrum.exe'
            - '\Bitcoin.exe'
            - '\Phantom.exe'
    condition: selection_path and not filter_legit
falsepositives:
    - Backup software
level: high
ENDPOINT

    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime
    from datamodel=Endpoint.Filesystem
    where (Filesystem.file_path="*\Ethereum\keystore\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Bitcoin\wallet.dat"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Exodus\exodus.wallet*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Electrum\wallets\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\MetaMask\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Phantom\*"
        OR Filesystem.file_path="*\Atomic\Local Storage\*")
      AND NOT Filesystem.process_name IN ("MetaMask.exe","Exodus.exe","Atomic.exe","electrum.exe","Bitcoin.exe","Phantom.exe")
    by Filesystem.dest, Filesystem.process_name, Filesystem.file_path, Filesystem.user
| `drop_dm_object_name(Filesystem)`
Phishing-link click correlated to endpoint execution Delivery High
User received an external email containing a URL, clicked it, and within 60 seconds the recipient's endpoint spawned a scripting interpreter or network downloader (PowerShell / cmd / mshta / curl / certutil / bitsadmin). This is the high-fidelity shape — a raw "user clicked an emailed URL" query is too noisy to alert on because every Microsoft 365 tenant generates thousands of legitimate clicks per day. The exec-correlation step suppresses the noise to roughly the rate at which a click actually drives onto-host execution, which is the actionable signal. Pair with UC_PHISH_LINK_BYPASS (Safe Links override) and UC_PHISH_LINK_NEWDOMAIN for orthogonal high-fidelity variants.
ATT&CKT1566.002 T1204.001 T1059.001
Data sourcesEmail.All_Email Web Endpoint.Processes EmailEvents EmailUrlInfo UrlClickEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s.
// Far higher fidelity than "every clicked URL" — most legitimate clicks
// never spawn a non-browser child process, so the join eliminates the
// 99% of noise that makes a raw click query unactionable.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectClicks = UrlClickEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | where ActionType in ("ClickAllowed","ClickedThrough")
    | join kind=inner (
        EmailEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
        | where DeliveryAction == "Delivered"
        | where EmailDirection == "Inbound"
        | project NetworkMessageId, Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain,
                  RecipientEmailAddress, EmailTimestamp = Timestamp
      ) on NetworkMessageId
    | join kind=leftouter (
        EmailUrlInfo | project NetworkMessageId, Url, UrlDomain
      ) on NetworkMessageId, Url
    | project ClickTime = Timestamp, AccountUpn, IPAddress, Url, UrlDomain,
              Subject, SenderFromAddress, SenderFromDomain, RecipientEmailAddress,
              ActionType;
// Correlate to a non-browser child process spawned within 60 seconds on
// the recipient's device.
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(LookbackDays)
| where InitiatingProcessFileName in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                         "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
| where FileName in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                        "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe","cscript.exe",
                        "bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe","curl.exe","wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectClicks on $left.AccountName == $right.AccountUpn
| where Timestamp between (ClickTime .. ClickTime + 60s)
| project ClickTime, ProcessTime = Timestamp,
          DelaySec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, ClickTime),
          DeviceName, AccountName, RecipientEmailAddress, SenderFromAddress,
          Subject, Url, UrlDomain, ActionType,
          FileName, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName
| order by ClickTime desc
// UrlClickEvents has no clean Sentinel-native equivalent. The detection
// pivots to: Office Send→Receive proximity to SecurityEvent 4688 of a
// non-browser child process. Lower fidelity than the Defender version.
let LookbackDays = 7d;
let SuspectMailUsers = OfficeActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
    | where OfficeWorkload == "Exchange"
    | where Operation =~ "Receive"
    | summarize Subject = any(Subject), Sender = any(Sender)
                by MailboxOwnerUPN, MessageId, EmailTime = bin(TimeGenerated, 1m);
SecurityEvent
| where TimeGenerated > ago(LookbackDays)
| where EventID == 4688
| where ParentProcessName has_any ("\chrome.exe","\msedge.exe",
                                     "\firefox.exe","\outlook.exe","\brave.exe")
| where NewProcessName has_any ("\powershell.exe","\pwsh.exe","\cmd.exe",
                                  "\mshta.exe","\rundll32.exe","\regsvr32.exe",
                                  "\wscript.exe","\cscript.exe","\bitsadmin.exe",
                                  "\certutil.exe","\curl.exe","\wget.exe")
| join kind=inner SuspectMailUsers on $left.Account == $right.MailboxOwnerUPN
| where TimeGenerated between (EmailTime .. EmailTime + 60s)
| project TimeGenerated, EmailTime, Computer, Account, ParentProcessName,
          NewProcessName, CommandLine, Sender, Subject
| order by TimeGenerated desc
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats `summariesonly` count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats `summariesonly` earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time
``` Phishing-link click that drives endpoint execution within 60s ```
| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS click_time
    from datamodel=Web
    where Web.action="allowed"
    by Web.src, Web.user, Web.dest, Web.url
| `drop_dm_object_name(Web)`
| rename user AS recipient, dest AS clicked_domain, url AS clicked_url
| join type=inner recipient
    [| tstats summariesonly=false count
         from datamodel=Email.All_Email
         where All_Email.action="delivered" AND All_Email.url!="-"
         by All_Email.recipient, All_Email.src_user, All_Email.url, All_Email.subject
     | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Email)`
     | rex field=url "https?://(?<email_domain>[^/]+)"
     | rename recipient AS recipient]
| join type=inner src
    [| tstats summariesonly=false earliest(_time) AS exec_time
         values(Processes.process) AS exec_cmd, values(Processes.process_name) AS exec_proc
         from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes
         where Processes.parent_process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe",
                                                   "outlook.exe","brave.exe","arc.exe")
           AND Processes.process_name IN ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","cmd.exe","mshta.exe",
                                            "rundll32.exe","regsvr32.exe","wscript.exe",
                                            "cscript.exe","bitsadmin.exe","certutil.exe",
                                            "curl.exe","wget.exe")
         by Processes.dest, Processes.user
     | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)`
     | rename dest AS src]
| eval delta_sec = exec_time - click_time
| where delta_sec >= 0 AND delta_sec <= 60
| table click_time, exec_time, delta_sec, recipient, src, src_user, subject,
        clicked_domain, clicked_url, exec_proc, exec_cmd
| sort - click_time

File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match Installation High
Match SHA256/SHA1/MD5 named in the article against EDR file/process telemetry.
ATT&CKT1027
Data sourcesEndpoint.Filesystem Endpoint.Processes DeviceFileEvents DeviceProcessEvents
// Pre-filter each table BEFORE the union — the engine cannot push
// predicates across `union`, and DeviceFileEvents lacks ProcessCommandLine
// (Initiating only) so column-projection has to differ per side.
union
    ( DeviceFileEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or SHA1 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or MD5 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceFileEvents" ),
    ( DeviceProcessEvents
        | where Timestamp > ago(7d)
        | where AccountName !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or SHA1 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb") or MD5 in~ ("37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb")
        | project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, FileName, FolderPath, SHA256,
                  Cmd = ProcessCommandLine, Source = "DeviceProcessEvents" )
| order by Timestamp desc

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["37f34aa3b86db6898065f3ca886031978580a15251f2576f6d24c3b778907336", "18f784b3bc9a0bcdcb1a8d7f51bc5f54323fc40cbd874119354ab609bef6e4cb"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Article-specific behavioural hunt — GopherWhisper: A burrow full of malware
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] whisper.dll loaded / svchost.exe spawned outside services.exe (GopherWhisper JabGopher injection) · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] Non-browser process posting to Slack Web API (LaxGopher C2) · [LLM] Non-browser process posting to Discord API (RatGopher C2) · [LLM] Beaconing to GopherWhisper C2 IP 43.231.113.50 (incl. SSLORDoor raw TLS/443) · [LLM] Suspicious draft email manipulation against barrantaya.1010@outlook.com (BoxOfFriends Graph API C2) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → [LLM] Outbound upload to file.io from non-browser process (CompactGopher exfil)
Non-browser process posting to Slack Web API (LaxGopher C2) AICommand & Control Medium
Hunts the LaxGopher Go-based backdoor's command-and-control channel, which polls a private Slack server via api.slack.com / hooks.slack.com from a non-browser, non-Slack-desktop process and publishes cmd.exe output back. Targets the article's specific C2 abuse pattern, not generic Slack traffic. Rationale: Article explicitly states LaxGopher 'interacts with a private Slack server to retrieve C&C messages [and] publishes the results back to the Slack channel configured in the code' — a Go binary calling Slack Web API from outside the Slack desktop client / browser is the exact attack fingerprint. The non-browser/non-Slack exclusion list keeps the noise floor low. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1102/002/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1071/001/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1102.002
Data sourcesWeb Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Internal devops/incident tools (Python/Go bots) posting to incoming webhooks at hooks.slack.com
  • CI runners (jenkins.exe, gitlab-runner.exe) calling Slack webhooks on build status
  • Custom-built Electron apps embedding Slack notifications
  • curl.exe/PowerShell used by sysadmins for one-off Slack notification scripts
  • SaaS productivity agents (e.g. Linear/Notion sync) calling Slack API
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files" and InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files (x86)" and InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("python.exe","pwsh.exe","curl.exe","jenkins.exe","gitlab-runner.exe")
Response runbook
1) Triage: enrich the InitiatingProcessFileName + folder path — confirm it is not a known sanctioned Slack-integration tool. Pull the binary hash; check VT and compare against the 8 SHA1s in the article (LaxGopher set). 2) Contain: isolate the host in Defender, block the binary by hash, kill the offending PID, and revoke the Slack token if a workspace token was found via netflow/PCAP. 3) Hunt: pivot on the same DeviceName for cmd.exe children of the binary (LaxGopher executes commands via cmd.exe), file writes to %TEMP%, and outbound DNS to discord.com / file.io / graph.microsoft.com from any process — GopherWhisper rotates C2 across all four.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where (RemoteUrl has_any ("slack.com/api/", "hooks.slack.com", "files.slack.com")) or RemoteUrl endswith ".slack.com"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","slack.exe","teams.exe","outlook.exe","ms-teams.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe")
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, RemoteUrl, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| summarize Hits = count(), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp), SampleUrl = any(RemoteUrl), SampleCmd = any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by LastSeen desc
let WindowMin = 5m;
let SlackEgress = ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where (isnotempty(Url) and Url has_any ("slack.com/api/", "hooks.slack.com", "files.slack.com")) or DstHostname endswith "slack.com"
    | project NetTime = TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, Url, DstHostname, DstIpAddr, NetworkBytes;
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","slack.exe","teams.exe","outlook.exe","ms-teams.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project ProcTime = TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername
| join kind=inner SlackEgress on DvcHostname
| where NetTime between (ProcTime .. ProcTime + WindowMin)
| project NetTime, ProcTime, DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername, Url, DstHostname, DstIpAddr, NetworkBytes
| order by NetTime desc
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:3 (@DestinationHostname:*slack.com OR @DestinationHostname:hooks.slack.com OR @DestinationHostname:files.slack.com) -@Image:(*\\chrome.exe OR *\\Chrome.exe OR *\\msedge.exe OR *\\firefox.exe OR *\\Firefox.exe OR *\\brave.exe OR *\\iexplore.exe OR *\\slack.exe OR *\\Slack.exe OR *\\teams.exe OR *\\Teams.exe OR *\\outlook.exe OR *\\OUTLOOK.EXE OR *\\opera.exe OR *\\vivaldi.exe)
#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/(^|\.)slack\.com$/i
| NOT ContextImageFileName=/\\(chrome|msedge|firefox|brave|iexplore|slack|teams|outlook|opera|vivaldi)\.exe$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, DomainName], function=count())
| count > 0
WEB
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, min(_time) as firstTime, max(_time) as lastTime, values(Web.url) as urls, values(Web.dest) as dests, dc(Web.url) as urlCount from datamodel=Web where (Web.url="*slack.com/api/*" OR Web.url="*hooks.slack.com*" OR Web.dest="*.slack.com") AND NOT (Web.app IN ("chrome","firefox","msedge","brave","iexplore","slack","teams","outlook","opera","vivaldi") OR Web.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","slack.exe","teams.exe","outlook.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe")) by Web.src, Web.user, Web.process_name, Web.app | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)` | sort - lastTime
| tstats summariesonly=false count, min(_time) as firstTime, max(_time) as lastTime, values(Web.url) as urls, values(Web.dest) as dests, dc(Web.url) as urlCount from datamodel=Web where (Web.url="*slack.com/api/*" OR Web.url="*hooks.slack.com*" OR Web.dest="*.slack.com") AND NOT (Web.app IN ("chrome","firefox","msedge","brave","iexplore","slack","teams","outlook","opera","vivaldi") OR Web.process_name IN ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","slack.exe","teams.exe","outlook.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe")) by Web.src, Web.user, Web.process_name, Web.app | `drop_dm_object_name(Web)` | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)` | sort - lastTime
Non-browser process posting to Discord API (RatGopher C2) AICommand & Control Medium
Hunts the RatGopher Go-based backdoor's C2, which polls a private Discord server channel via discord.com / discordapp.com from a non-browser, non-Discord-desktop process. Same operator playbook as LaxGopher but on a different platform. Rationale: Article: RatGopher 'interacts with a private Discord server to retrieve C&C messages [and] on successful execution of a command, the results are published back to the configured Discord channel.' Discord traffic from a non-Discord-desktop, non-browser process on a government endpoint in Mongolia is anomalous; the destination filter is article-specific. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1102/002/ • https://attack.mitre.org/software/S0608/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1102.002
Data sourcesWeb Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Game launchers (Steam.exe, EpicGamesLauncher.exe) embedding Discord Rich Presence
  • Custom Electron apps embedding Discord-SDK
  • Internal devops bots posting to Discord webhooks (discord.com/api/webhooks/...)
  • OBS Studio / streaming tools with Discord integration
  • Personal use of Discord PWA via msedge.exe (already excluded) versus a chromium-based wrapper not on the list
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("steam.exe","epicgameslauncher.exe","obs64.exe","obs32.exe","streamlabs obs.exe") and InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files"
Response runbook
1) Triage: confirm the InitiatingProcessFileName is not a sanctioned tool. Check the binary against the 8 SHA1 IOCs in the article. Pull netflow to identify the destination Discord channel/guild id from URL path if TLS decrypt is in place. 2) Contain: isolate device, kill PID, block process hash, request Discord T&S takedown of the actor channel with the URL pivot. 3) Hunt: search the same host for cmd.exe children of the binary and concurrent Slack/file.io/graph.microsoft.com traffic — GopherWhisper rotates across all four.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteUrl has_any ("discord.com/api/", "discordapp.com/api/", "discord.com", "discordapp.com")
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","discord.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe","electron.exe")
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| summarize Hits = count(), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp), SampleUrl = any(RemoteUrl), SampleCmd = any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by LastSeen desc
let WindowMin = 5m;
let DiscordEgress = ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
    | where (isnotempty(Url) and Url has_any ("discord.com/api/", "discordapp.com/api/")) or DstHostname in~ ("discord.com","discordapp.com") or DstHostname endswith ".discord.com"
    | project NetTime = TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, Url, DstHostname, DstIpAddr, NetworkBytes;
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","discord.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe","electron.exe")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project ProcTime = TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername
| join kind=inner DiscordEgress on DvcHostname
| where NetTime between (ProcTime .. ProcTime + WindowMin)
| project NetTime, ProcTime, DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername, Url, DstHostname, DstIpAddr, NetworkBytes
| order by NetTime desc
source:windows.sysmon @EventID:3 (@DestinationHostname:*discord.com OR @DestinationHostname:*discordapp.com) -@Image:(*\\chrome.exe OR *\\Chrome.exe OR *\\msedge.exe OR *\\firefox.exe OR *\\Firefox.exe OR *\\brave.exe OR *\\iexplore.exe OR *\\discord.exe OR *\\Discord.exe OR *\\opera.exe OR *\\vivaldi.exe)
#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/(^|\.)(discord|discordapp)\.com$/i
| NOT ContextImageFileName=/\\(chrome|msedge|firefox|brave|iexplore|discord|opera|vivaldi|electron)\.exe$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, DomainName], function=count())
| count > 0

Outbound upload to file.io from non-browser process (CompactGopher exfil) AIActions on Objectives High
Hunts the CompactGopher Go-based exfiltration tool, which compresses collected files from the command line and uploads them to the file.io public file-sharing service. Detection is article-specific: file.io is an unusual destination for a government workstation. Rationale: Article names file.io explicitly: 'CompactGopher [...] automatically exfiltrates them to the file.io file sharing service.' file.io is not a typical destination on a government workstation, so traffic from any non-browser process to file.io is high fidelity. Cross-checked with MITRE T1567.002 (Exfil to Cloud Storage). Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1567/002/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1560/
ATT&CKT1567.002 T1560
Data sourcesWeb Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Employees using file.io personally via browser (already excluded by filter)
  • Developer tooling (Postman, Insomnia) used to test file.io API
  • Curl/wget by sysadmins for legitimate file sharing
  • Backup scripts uploading to file.io (rare in gov but possible)
  • Penetration test exercises uploading test payloads
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files" and InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("postman.exe","insomnia.exe","curl.exe")
Response runbook
1) Triage: inspect the cmdline for archive flags (zip/tar/7z names) and the parent process tree — CompactGopher is usually a payload dropped by LaxGopher. Pull the binary hash and check against the 8 SHA1 IOCs in the article. 2) Contain: isolate host, block destination at egress proxy/firewall, kill PID, snapshot memory for the dropped Go binary. 3) Hunt: enumerate files recently archived/compressed on the host (file create events for .zip/.7z/.tar.gz in %TEMP% or %APPDATA%), and check the same DeviceId for prior Slack/Discord/Graph C2 hits — file.io use implies an earlier-stage backdoor told it to exfil.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has "file.io" or RemoteUrl endswith ".file.io"
| where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe")
| where InitiatingProcessAccountName !endswith "$"
| summarize Hits = count(), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp), SampleCmd = any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine), DstIPs = make_set(RemoteIP, 5) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by LastSeen desc
let WindowMin = 5m;
let FileIoEgress = ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where (isnotempty(Url) and Url has "file.io") or DstHostname =~ "file.io" or DstHostname endswith ".file.io"
    | project NetTime = TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, Url, DstHostname, DstIpAddr, NetworkBytes;
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where ActingProcessName !in~ ("chrome.exe","msedge.exe","firefox.exe","brave.exe","iexplore.exe","opera.exe","vivaldi.exe")
| where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
| project ProcTime = TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername
| join kind=inner FileIoEgress on DvcHostname
| where NetTime between (ProcTime .. ProcTime + WindowMin)
| project NetTime, ProcTime, DvcHostname, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, ActorUsername, Url, DstHostname, DstIpAddr, NetworkBytes
| order by NetTime desc
title: CompactGopher exfiltration to file.io (GopherWhisper APT)
id: 7e3a5c1b-4f8d-46a7-9b2c-5d1e6f0a2b3c
description: Outbound connection to file.io public file-sharing service from a non-browser process — used by GopherWhisper's CompactGopher Go-based exfiltration tool to upload compressed archives of stolen data.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/gopherwhisper-burrow-full-malware/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.exfiltration
  - attack.t1567.002
  - attack.t1560
logsource:
  category: network_connection
  product: windows
detection:
  selection_dst:
    DestinationHostname|endswith:
      - 'file.io'
  filter_browser:
    Image|endswith:
      - '\chrome.exe'
      - '\msedge.exe'
      - '\firefox.exe'
      - '\brave.exe'
      - '\iexplore.exe'
      - '\opera.exe'
      - '\vivaldi.exe'
  condition: selection_dst and not filter_browser
level: high

source:windows.sysmon @EventID:3 (@DestinationHostname:file.io OR @DestinationHostname:*.file.io) -@Image:(*\\chrome.exe OR *\\Chrome.exe OR *\\msedge.exe OR *\\firefox.exe OR *\\Firefox.exe OR *\\brave.exe OR *\\iexplore.exe OR *\\opera.exe OR *\\vivaldi.exe)
#event_simpleName=DnsRequest DomainName=/^file\.io$/i
| NOT ContextImageFileName=/\\(chrome|msedge|firefox|brave|iexplore|opera|vivaldi)\.exe$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ContextImageFileName, DomainName], function=count())
| count > 0
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, min(_time) as firstTime, max(_time) as lastTime, sum(All_Traffic.bytes_out) as bytes_out, values(All_Traffic.dest) as dests from datamodel=Network_Traffic where (All_Traffic.dest="file.io" OR All_Traffic.dest="*.file.io") by All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.user, All_Traffic.app, All_Traffic.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)` | where bytes_out > 100000 | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)` | sort - bytes_out
| tstats summariesonly=false count, min(_time) as firstTime, max(_time) as lastTime, sum(All_Traffic.bytes_out) as bytes_out, values(All_Traffic.dest) as dests from datamodel=Network_Traffic where (All_Traffic.dest="file.io" OR All_Traffic.dest="*.file.io") by All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.user, All_Traffic.app, All_Traffic.process_name | `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)` | where bytes_out > 100000 | `security_content_ctime(firstTime)` | `security_content_ctime(lastTime)` | sort - bytes_out
Beaconing to GopherWhisper C2 IP 43.231.113.50 (incl. SSLORDoor raw TLS/443) AICommand & Control High
Hunts contact with the GopherWhisper C2 IP 43.231.113.50 published by ESET, including SSLORDoor's raw-socket TCP/443 channel built on OpenSSL BIO. Hard IOC — any contact is a strong signal. Rationale: 43.231.113.50 is the IP IOC published by ESET for GopherWhisper. Any traffic to/from this IP is a hard-IOC alert. Single-event-shape, so Sigma is appropriate. Cross-referenced with the article's SSLORDoor description (TCP/443 raw sockets via OpenSSL BIO). Cross-checked against: • https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/gopherwhisper-burrow-full-malware/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1095/
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1095
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • IP reassignment to a benign CDN/cloud tenant in the future — re-validate quarterly against current reputation
  • Threat intel sweeps from internal scanners may originate traffic to this IP (e.g. Censys/Shodan tooling)
  • Pentest team explicitly contacting this IP for IOC validation
  • VPN egress shared with another tenant generating noise (very rare)
  • Researcher's malware sandbox detonation in scope of corporate network
Response runbook
1) Triage: identify all hosts that contacted 43.231.113.50 in the last 90 days; pull initiating process and account on each. Cross-reference against the 8 SHA1 IOCs from the article — that confirms GopherWhisper malware presence vs incidental contact. 2) Contain: blackhole 43.231.113.50 at all egress points (proxy, firewall, DNS RPZ); isolate confirmed hosts; revoke credentials of any AccountName that ran the offending process. 3) Hunt: on every confirmed host check for whisper.dll on disk, suspicious svchost.exe spawned outside services.exe parent (JabGopher injection), and concurrent C2 over Slack/Discord/Graph/file.io — GopherWhisper rotates C2 channels.
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(90d)
| where RemoteIP == "43.231.113.50" or LocalIP == "43.231.113.50"
| summarize Hits = count(), FirstSeen = min(Timestamp), LastSeen = max(Timestamp), DestPorts = make_set(RemotePort, 10), SampleCmd = any(InitiatingProcessCommandLine), Procs = make_set(InitiatingProcessFileName, 10) by DeviceName, InitiatingProcessAccountName
| order by LastSeen desc
union isfuzzy=true
(CommonSecurityLog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where DestinationIP == "43.231.113.50" or SourceIP == "43.231.113.50"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = coalesce(SourceHostName, DestinationHostName), RemoteIP = iff(DestinationIP == "43.231.113.50", DestinationIP, SourceIP), RemotePort = DestinationPort, Process = SourceServiceName, Vendor = DeviceVendor, Bytes = SentBytes),
(ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(90d)
    | where DstIpAddr == "43.231.113.50" or SrcIpAddr == "43.231.113.50"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host = DvcHostname, RemoteIP = iff(DstIpAddr == "43.231.113.50", DstIpAddr, SrcIpAddr), RemotePort = DstPortNumber, Process = SrcUsername, Vendor = EventVendor, Bytes = NetworkBytes)
| summarize Hits = count(), FirstSeen = min(TimeGenerated), LastSeen = max(TimeGenerated), Ports = make_set(RemotePort, 10), TotalBytes = sum(Bytes) by Host, RemoteIP, Vendor
| order by LastSeen desc

Suspicious draft email manipulation against barrantaya.1010@outlook.com (BoxOfFriends Graph API C2) AICommand & Control Medium
Hunts the BoxOfFriends Go backdoor's C2 channel: Microsoft Graph API calls that create/modify drafts in the attacker mailbox barrantaya.1010@outlook.com (created 11 July 2024, used by the loader FriendDelivery from 22 July 2024). Either the IOC mailbox shows up in O365 traffic, or a non-Office process contacts graph.microsoft.com. Rationale: Article gives a hard mailbox IOC barrantaya.1010@outlook.com plus the technique: 'BoxOfFriends [...] makes use of the Microsoft 365 Outlook mail REST API from Microsoft Graph to create and modify draft email messages for its C&C communications.' Two-pronged hunt: the literal mailbox IOC in mail-flow logs, plus non-Office processes touching graph.microsoft.com (which is what a Go-language backdoor would look like). Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1102/002/ • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/graph/api/user-post-messages
ATT&CKT1102.002 T1071.001 T1567
Data sourcesWeb Network_Traffic.All_Traffic Email
Expected false positives · estimated rate: medium — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Custom internal scripts using Graph API to send/read mail (PowerShell + MSAL.PS, python msgraph-sdk)
  • Backup/archival tooling (Veeam, MailStore) reading mailboxes via Graph
  • Mobile device management agents on Windows endpoints
  • Power Automate desktop flows touching Graph
  • Microsoft Authenticator companion services
Suppression filter
| where ActingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Program Files" and ActingProcessName !in~ ("powershell.exe","pwsh.exe","python.exe","backup-runner.exe","veeam.backup.shell.exe")
Response runbook
1) Triage: if mailbox IOC fires, treat as direct hit — pull all messages to/from barrantaya.1010@outlook.com across the tenant via eDiscovery and review for draft-message C2 patterns (base64 or compressed payload in body). If Graph-from-non-Office fires, confirm InitiatingProcessFileName against the SHA1 IOC list. 2) Contain: block the external sender at transport rule, revoke and force-rotate the Graph access token for any user that authenticated the offending process. Isolate the host. 3) Hunt: pivot to the same host for Slack/Discord/file.io egress (UC1–3) and for FriendDelivery DLL presence (filename pivot in DeviceFileEvents).

whisper.dll loaded / svchost.exe spawned outside services.exe (GopherWhisper JabGopher injection) AIInstallation High
Hunts the JabGopher injector chain: a fresh svchost.exe process spawned by a non-services.exe parent (anomalous; legitimate svchost is always a child of services.exe), and/or the whisper.dll module load — the DLL filename ESET attributes LaxGopher to. Together these flag JabGopher injecting LaxGopher into svchost. Rationale: Article: 'JabGopher [...] creates a new instance of svchost.exe and injects LaxGopher into the svchost.exe process memory' and the side-loaded DLL filename is whisper.dll (the article-named binary). Legitimate svchost.exe is always parented by services.exe (with the rare wininit/MsMpEng exceptions). Two independent anchor strings — the parent anomaly and the whisper.dll filename — gives a very high-fidelity detection. Cross-checked against: • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1055/012/ • https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1574/002/ • https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/windows/win32/services/svchost
ATT&CKT1055.012 T1574.002 T1036.005
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes Endpoint.Filesystem
Expected false positives · estimated rate: low — model-assessed, tune to your environment
  • Forensic / red-team tooling that re-launches svchost.exe under PsExec for testing (parent will be PSEXESVC)
  • Buggy in-house installers spawning svchost.exe for service registration
  • Legitimate DLLs literally named whisper.dll from a third-party app (Whisper.ai voice apps load model files but typically not a DLL of that name in System32)
  • Defender / EDR validation labs running attack simulations
  • Microsoft Edge WebView2 spawning svchost-like processes (rare; usually distinguishable by folder path)
Suppression filter
| where InitiatingProcessParentFileName !endswith "\\PSEXESVC.EXE" and InitiatingProcessFolderPath !startswith @"C:\Windows\System32\" and ProcessCommandLine !contains "WinDefend"
Response runbook
1) Triage: capture the anomalous svchost.exe PID and pull memory; check whisper.dll path on disk against the 8 SHA1 IOCs listed (c72e7540..., 039eb329..., etc.). Map the parent process — that is JabGopher; submit its hash to VT and EDR repo. 2) Contain: kill svchost PID and parent, isolate host in EDR, block both file hashes (JabGopher + whisper.dll) tenant-wide, snapshot disk for full forensics. 3) Hunt: search the same host's outbound for Slack/Discord/Graph/file.io C2 (UCs 1–3, 5) and for the 43.231.113.50 IP (UC 4). Pivot on JabGopher hash across the estate for prior staging.
union isfuzzy=true
(DeviceProcessEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName =~ "svchost.exe"
    | where InitiatingProcessFileName !in~ ("services.exe","msmpeng.exe","wininit.exe")
    | where AccountName !endswith "$"
    | project Timestamp, Source = "AnomalousSvchost", DeviceName, AccountName, ParentImage = InitiatingProcessFolderPath, ParentCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, ChildImage = FolderPath, ChildCmd = ProcessCommandLine, SHA1, SHA256),
(DeviceImageLoadEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName =~ "whisper.dll"
    | project Timestamp, Source = "WhisperDllLoad", DeviceName, ChildImage = InitiatingProcessFileName, ChildPath = InitiatingProcessFolderPath, ChildCmd = InitiatingProcessCommandLine, LoadedPath = FolderPath, SHA1, SHA256),
(DeviceFileEvents
    | where Timestamp > ago(30d)
    | where FileName =~ "whisper.dll" and ActionType in ("FileCreated","FileRenamed","FileModified")

| order by Timestamp desc

    | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"

(ImFileEvent
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetFileName =~ "whisper.dll"
    | project TimeGenerated, Source = "WhisperDllFileEvent", DvcHostname, ActorUsername, ActingProcessName, ActingProcessCommandLine, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256)
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: GopherWhisper whisper.dll loaded by svchost.exe (LaxGopher injection)
id: 5b6c9d2e-3f4a-5b6c-9d0e-1f2a3b4c5d6e
description: Suspicious image load of whisper.dll by svchost.exe. ESET attributes this DLL to GopherWhisper's LaxGopher backdoor, side-loaded after JabGopher creates a fresh svchost.exe and injects into its memory.
references:
  - https://www.welivesecurity.com/en/eset-research/gopherwhisper-burrow-full-malware/
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/20
tags:
  - attack.defense_evasion
  - attack.t1574.002
  - attack.t1055.012
  - attack.t1036.005
logsource:
  category: image_load
  product: windows
detection:
  selection:
    Image|endswith: '\svchost.exe'
    ImageLoaded|endswith: '\whisper.dll'
  condition: selection
level: high

(source:windows.sysmon @EventID:7 @Image:(*\\svchost.exe OR *\\SVCHOST.EXE OR *\\Svchost.exe) @ImageLoaded:(*\\whisper.dll OR *\\Whisper.dll OR *\\WHISPER.DLL)) OR (source:windows.sysmon @EventID:1 @Image:(*\\svchost.exe OR *\\Svchost.exe) -@ParentImage:(*\\services.exe OR *\\Services.exe OR *\\MsMpEng.exe OR *\\wininit.exe OR *\\Wininit.exe)) OR (source:windows.security @EventID:4688 @Image:(*\\svchost.exe OR *\\Svchost.exe) -@ParentProcessName:(*\\services.exe OR *\\Services.exe OR *\\wininit.exe))
#event_simpleName=ProcessRollup2 ImageFileName=/\\svchost\.exe$/i
| NOT ParentBaseFileName=/^(services|MsMpEng|wininit)\.exe$/i
| groupBy([aid, ComputerName, ParentBaseFileName, ParentImageFileName, CommandLine, SHA256HashData], function=count())
| count > 0

Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time

| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

// Pre-filter both ASIM tables before the union — same pattern as the
// Defender version so the engine can push hash predicates per-side.
let BadHashes = dynamic(["c72e7540d6f12d74d8e737b02f31568385f575d7", "039eb329a173fce7efeca18611a8f2c0f7d24609", "716554dc580a82cc17a1035add302c0766590964", "57c2490e4db194d3503ee85635fb1d6f26e8c534", "ad7e264eb08415871617e45f21d03f7d71e4c36f", "fa9e65e58eb8fa41fde0a0a870b7d24b298026d9", "5a1bbb40c442b12594a913431f8c6757a3a66e8f", "926974facfd0383c65458d6ef1f31fbb7c769e18"]);
union
    ( ImFileEvent
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where TargetFileSHA256 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileSHA1 in~ (BadHashes)
           or TargetFileMD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType,
                  TargetFileName, TargetFilePath, TargetFileSHA256,
                  Cmd = ActingProcessName, Source = "ImFileEvent" ),
    ( ImProcessCreate
        | where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
        | where ActorUsername !endswith "$"
        | where SHA256 in~ (BadHashes) or SHA1 in~ (BadHashes) or MD5 in~ (BadHashes)
        | project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, EventType = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileName = TargetProcessName, TargetFilePath = TargetProcessName,
                  TargetFileSHA256 = SHA256,
                  Cmd = TargetProcessCommandLine, Source = "ImProcessCreate" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc

| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count

    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic

title: Trusted Vendor Binary Launching Unusual Children
id: 7c8b2e10-4d4f-4a8c-9c1f-bf6e2c4a8d09
status: experimental
description: |
    A signed vendor binary (in the article's named-vendors list)
    spawning script-host or LOLBin children. Strongest signal when
    paired with a recent CVE / supply-chain advisory naming the same
    vendor.
references:
    - https://attack.mitre.org/techniques/T1195/
author: Clankerusecase
date: 2026/05/02
tags:
    - attack.t1195
    - attack.initial-access
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: windows
detection:
    selection_child:
        Image|endswith:
            - '\powershell.exe'
            - '\pwsh.exe'
            - '\cmd.exe'
            - '\rundll32.exe'
            - '\regsvr32.exe'
            - '\mshta.exe'
            - '\wscript.exe'
            - '\cscript.exe'
            - '\wmic.exe'
            - '\bitsadmin.exe'
    selection_parent_vendor:
        ParentImage|contains:
            - '\Program Files\'
            - '\Program Files (x86)\'
    condition: selection_child and selection_parent_vendor
falsepositives:
    - Many — installers and updaters legitimately spawn children. Pin
      the parent path to specific vendor binaries observed in the
      article before alerting.
level: medium

Data sourcesVulnerabilities DeviceInfo DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
// TVM tables are daily snapshots — pin a 1-day window so the engine
// partitions on Timestamp instead of scanning the full retention.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-39987")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where Timestamp > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId, VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

// Sentinel has no native TVM table. If MDE→Sentinel streaming is enabled,
// `DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities` is available. Otherwise this UC has
// no Sentinel translation — track via Update / Heartbeat OS-version
// gaps as a coarse approximation.
DeviceTvmSoftwareVulnerabilities
| where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)
| where CveId in~ ("CVE-2026-39987")
| join kind=inner (DeviceInfo | where TimeGenerated > ago(1d)) on DeviceId
| project DeviceName, OSPlatform, CveId,
          VulnerabilitySeverityLevel, RecommendedSecurityUpdate
| order by VulnerabilitySeverityLevel asc, DeviceName asc

Actions on Objectives
Detected
Reconnaissance. Attacker researches the target — OSINT, scanning, enumeration.
Weaponization. Coupling exploit code with deliverable payload (e.g. weaponized PDF/Office doc, trojanized installer).
Delivery. Transmitting the weapon (phishing email, USB, watering hole, drive-by, malicious ad).
Exploitation. Triggering the exploit — vulnerability/macro/trust abuse to execute code. → Trusted vendor binary / installer launching unusual children · Article-specific behavioural hunt — GPT-Proxy Backdoor in npm and PyPI turns Servers into Chinese LLM Relays
Installation. Establishing persistence on the host — backdoor, service, scheduled task, registry key. → [LLM] Stage-2 implant masquerading as node-health-check daemon (/tmp/.kh, /tmp/.ns) · [LLM] npm/PyPI dropper self-cleanup: find rm -rf of kube-health-tools in node_modules · Suspicious browser extension installation · File hash IOCs — endpoint file/process match
Command & Control. Beacon to attacker infrastructure for control and tasking. → [LLM] GPT-Proxy backdoor C2 / Stage-2 download (sync.geeker.indevs.in, gibunxi4201/kube-node-diag) · Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations · Network connections to article IPs / domains
Actions on Objectives. Lateral movement, credential dumping, data theft, ransomware, sabotage. → Infostealer — non-browser process accessing browser cookie/login DBs · Crypto-wallet file/keystore access by non-wallet process
GPT-Proxy backdoor C2 / Stage-2 download (sync.geeker.indevs.in, gibunxi4201/kube-node-diag) AICommand & Control High
Detects Linux hosts resolving the GPT-Proxy backdoor C2 domain sync.geeker.indevs.in or pulling the stage-2 implant from the gibunxi4201/kube-node-diag GitHub release. Both indicators are unique to this campaign — there is no benign reason for a workload to touch either. Rationale: sync.geeker.indevs.in and the gibunxi4201 GitHub account are unique campaign IOCs cited in the Aikido article — both backed by file hashes (b3405b8456…, 5d58ce3119…, fb3ae78d09…, 3a3d8f8636…) so a single hit is high-fidelity. Article corroborates the AhnLab honeypot reporting on Chinese proxy-tool deployment as the broader behavioural pattern. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/gpt-proxy-backdoor-npm-pypi-chinese-llm-relay • https://github.com/gibunxi4201/kube-node-diag
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1105 T1572
Data sourcesNetwork_Resolution.DNS Web.Web Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
// Defender for Endpoint on Linux
let c2_host = "sync.geeker.indevs.in";
let stage2_path = "gibunxi4201/kube-node-diag";
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where RemoteUrl has c2_host
    or RemoteUrl has stage2_path
    or RemoteUrl has "kube-diag-linux-amd64-packed"
    or RemoteUrl has "kube-diag-full-linux-amd64-packed"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, RemoteIP, RemoteUrl, RemotePort, Protocol
| order by Timestamp desc
let c2_host = "sync.geeker.indevs.in";
let stage2_path = "gibunxi4201/kube-node-diag";
union isfuzzy=true
( ImDnsActivity
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DnsQuery has c2_host or DnsQuery endswith ".geeker.indevs.in"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, Indicator=DnsQuery, Source="DNS" ),
( ImWebSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where Url has stage2_path or Url has "kube-diag-linux-amd64-packed" or Url has "kube-diag-full-linux-amd64-packed" or HttpHostName has c2_host
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, Indicator=Url, Source="Web" ),
( ImNetworkSession
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where DstHostname has c2_host
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, SrcIpAddr, Indicator=DstHostname, Source="NetworkSession" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc

Stage-2 implant masquerading as node-health-check daemon (/tmp/.kh, /tmp/.ns) AIInstallation High
Detects execution of the GPT-Proxy stage-2 Go binary from its hardcoded drop locations /tmp/.kh or /tmp/.ns, or any process renamed to 'node-health-check --mode=daemon' (the disguise string baked into the implant config). Also matches on the four known SHA-256 hashes from the article. Rationale: All four indicator classes are pulled from the Aikido article: stage-2 drop paths /tmp/.kh and /tmp/.ns (article §Stage 1), exact filenames kube-diag-linux-amd64-packed / kube-diag-full-linux-amd64-packed (article IOC table), the 'process_name: node-health-check, argv: --mode=daemon' disguise block from the decrypted XOR config, and four SHA-256 hashes from the IOC table. Any single match is enough — these are campaign-unique strings. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/gpt-proxy-backdoor-npm-pypi-chinese-llm-relay
ATT&CKT1036.004 T1036.005 T1059.004 T1564.001
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
let known_hashes = dynamic(["b3405b8456f4e82f192cdff6fdd5b290a58fafda01fbc08174105b922bd7b3cf","5d58ce3119c37f2bd552f4d883a4f4896dfcb8fb04875f844f999497e4ca846d","fb3ae78d09c119ec335c3b99a95c97d9bb6f92fd2c7c9b0d3e875347e2f25bb2","3a3d8f8636fa1db21871005a49ecd7fa59688fa763622fa737ce6b899558b300"]);
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FolderPath startswith "/tmp/.kh"
    or FolderPath startswith "/tmp/.ns"
    or InitiatingProcessFolderPath startswith "/tmp/.kh"
    or InitiatingProcessFolderPath startswith "/tmp/.ns"
    or FileName in ("kube-diag-linux-amd64-packed","kube-diag-full-linux-amd64-packed")
    or (ProcessCommandLine has "node-health-check" and ProcessCommandLine has "--mode=daemon")
    or SHA256 in (known_hashes)
    or InitiatingProcessSHA256 in (known_hashes)
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, SHA256, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessFolderPath, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
| order by Timestamp desc
let known_hashes = dynamic(["b3405b8456f4e82f192cdff6fdd5b290a58fafda01fbc08174105b922bd7b3cf","5d58ce3119c37f2bd552f4d883a4f4896dfcb8fb04875f844f999497e4ca846d","fb3ae78d09c119ec335c3b99a95c97d9bb6f92fd2c7c9b0d3e875347e2f25bb2","3a3d8f8636fa1db21871005a49ecd7fa59688fa763622fa737ce6b899558b300"]);
ImProcessCreate
| where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
| where TargetProcessName has "/tmp/.kh"
    or TargetProcessName has "/tmp/.ns"
    or TargetProcessName endswith "/kube-diag-linux-amd64-packed"
    or TargetProcessName endswith "/kube-diag-full-linux-amd64-packed"
    or (TargetProcessCommandLine has "node-health-check" and TargetProcessCommandLine has "--mode=daemon")
    or SHA256 in (known_hashes)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, ActorUsername, TargetProcessName, TargetProcessCommandLine, ParentProcessName, ParentProcessCommandLine, SHA256
| order by TimeGenerated desc

id: b2c3d4e5-f6a7-4b8c-9d0e-1f2a3b4c5d6e
status: experimental
description: Detects the GPT-Proxy stage-2 Chisel/SOCKS5/LLM-proxy Go binary executing from /tmp/.kh or /tmp/.ns, or any process renamed to 'node-health-check --mode=daemon' (the hardcoded disguise string in the kube-health-tools npm / kube-node-health PyPI dropper config).
references:

author: SOC Detection Engineering

npm/PyPI dropper self-cleanup: find rm -rf of kube-health-tools in node_modules AIInstallation High
Detects the GPT-Proxy dropper's self-deletion routine: a find command scanning the filesystem for the kube-health-tools (or kube-node-health) package directory under node_modules and recursively rm-rf-ing it within ~2 seconds of stage-2 launch. The exact cmdline shape ('find / -type d -name kube-health-tools -path */node_modules/* -exec rm -rf {} +') is a campaign-unique anti-forensic artefact even if the C2 has rotated. Rationale: Cmdline string 'find / -type d -name "kube-health-tools" -path "*/node_modules/*" -exec rm -rf {} +' is quoted verbatim from the Aikido article (article §Stage 1 cleanup snippet). The package-name + node_modules + rm-rf triple is a unique self-cleanup shape — generic find/rm hunts on Linux would FP heavily, but pinning to the campaign package names makes this alerting-tier. Survives hash rotation since this is a behaviour, not a binary. Cross-checked against: • https://www.aikido.dev/blog/gpt-proxy-backdoor-npm-pypi-chinese-llm-relay
ATT&CKT1070.004 T1195.002
Data sourcesEndpoint.Processes
DeviceProcessEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(30d)
| where FileName =~ "find"
| where ProcessCommandLine has_any ("kube-health-tools","kube-node-health")
| where ProcessCommandLine has "node_modules"
| where ProcessCommandLine has "rm -rf" or ProcessCommandLine has "-exec rm"
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, AccountName, FileName, FolderPath, ProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine, InitiatingProcessFolderPath
| order by Timestamp desc
union isfuzzy=true
( ImProcessCreate
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where TargetProcessName endswith "/find"
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has_any ("kube-health-tools","kube-node-health")
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has "node_modules"
    | where TargetProcessCommandLine has "rm -rf" or TargetProcessCommandLine has "-exec rm"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=DvcHostname, User=ActorUsername, Cmd=TargetProcessCommandLine, Parent=ParentProcessName, ParentCmd=ParentProcessCommandLine ),
( Syslog
    | where TimeGenerated > ago(30d)
    | where ProcessName =~ "audispd" or Facility =~ "auth" or Facility =~ "user"
    | where SyslogMessage has "find" and SyslogMessage has_any ("kube-health-tools","kube-node-health") and SyslogMessage has "node_modules" and SyslogMessage has "rm"
    | project TimeGenerated, Host=Computer, User="", Cmd=SyslogMessage, Parent="", ParentCmd="" )
| order by TimeGenerated desc
title: GPT-Proxy npm/PyPI Dropper Self-Cleanup via find rm -rf in node_modules
id: c3d4e5f6-a7b8-4c9d-9e1f-2a3b4c5d6e7f
status: experimental
description: Detects the GPT-Proxy backdoor dropper's anti-forensic self-deletion — a find scan for the kube-health-tools (npm) or kube-node-health (PyPI) package directory under node_modules, then recursive rm -rf. The dropper executes this within 2 seconds of stage-2 launch so the package directory is gone before incident-response inventories run.
references:
    - https://www.aikido.dev/blog/gpt-proxy-backdoor-npm-pypi-chinese-llm-relay
author: SOC Detection Engineering
date: 2026/05/13
tags:
    - attack.defense_evasion
    - attack.t1070.004
    - attack.initial_access
    - attack.t1195.002
logsource:
    category: process_creation
    product: linux
detection:
    selection_find:
        Image|endswith: '/find'
    selection_pkg:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'kube-health-tools'
            - 'kube-node-health'
    selection_modules:
        CommandLine|contains: 'node_modules'
    selection_delete:
        CommandLine|contains:
            - 'rm -rf'
            - '-exec rm'
    condition: selection_find and selection_pkg and selection_modules and selection_delete
falsepositives:
    - Legitimate cleanup scripts by SOC / DevOps responding to this very advisory.
level: high
source:(linux.auditd OR linux.syslog) @process.name:find @process.command_line:(*kube-health-tools* OR *kube-node-health*) @process.command_line:*node_modules* @process.command_line:(*"rm -rf"* OR *"-exec rm"*)
ENDPOINT
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats summariesonly=true count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as cmdline values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.process_name="find" AND (Processes.process="*kube-health-tools*" OR Processes.process="*kube-node-health*") AND Processes.process="*/node_modules/*" AND Processes.process="*rm -rf*" by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
| tstats summariesonly=false count min(_time) as firstTime max(_time) as lastTime values(Processes.process) as cmdline values(Processes.parent_process_name) as parent from datamodel=Endpoint.Processes where Processes.process_name="find" AND (Processes.process="*kube-health-tools*" OR Processes.process="*kube-node-health*") AND Processes.process="*/node_modules/*" AND Processes.process="*rm -rf*" by Processes.dest, Processes.user, Processes.process_path | `drop_dm_object_name(Processes)` | convert ctime(firstTime) ctime(lastTime)
Beaconing — periodic outbound to small set of destinations Command & Control Medium
C2 channel detection via inter-beacon-time stddev / fan-out to single dest.
ATT&CKT1071.001 T1071.004
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(1d)
| where RemoteIPType == "Public" and ActionType == "ConnectionSuccess"
| project DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort, Timestamp
| sort by DeviceName asc, RemoteIP asc, RemotePort asc, Timestamp asc
| extend prev_dev = prev(DeviceName, 1), prev_ip = prev(RemoteIP, 1),
         prev_port = prev(RemotePort, 1), prev_ts = prev(Timestamp, 1)
| where DeviceName == prev_dev and RemoteIP == prev_ip and RemotePort == prev_port
| extend delta_sec = datetime_diff('second', Timestamp, prev_ts)
| summarize conn_count = count(), avg_delta = avg(delta_sec), stdev_delta = stdev(delta_sec)
    by DeviceName, RemoteIP, RemotePort
| where conn_count > 30 and avg_delta between (30.0 .. 600.0) and stdev_delta < 5.0
| order by conn_count desc
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(2h)
| where DvcAction != "Block"
| where ipv4_is_private(DstIpAddr) == false
| summarize ConnCount = count(),
            DistinctMinutes = dcount(bin(TimeGenerated, 1m))
            by DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber
| where ConnCount > 50 and DistinctMinutes > 30   // sustained, not bursty
| order by ConnCount desc
NETWORK
Toggle if your env has no CIM data-model acceleration.
| tstats `summariesonly` count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
| tstats summariesonly=false count, values(All_Traffic.dest_port) AS ports
    from datamodel=Network_Traffic.All_Traffic
    where All_Traffic.action="allowed" AND All_Traffic.dest_category!="internal"
    by _time span=10s, All_Traffic.src, All_Traffic.dest
| `drop_dm_object_name(All_Traffic)`
| streamstats current=f last(_time) AS prev_time by src, dest
| eval delta = _time - prev_time
| stats avg(delta) AS avg_delta stdev(delta) AS sd_delta count by src, dest
| where count > 30 AND sd_delta < 5 AND avg_delta>=30 AND avg_delta<=600
| sort - count
Network connections to article IPs / domains Command & Control High
Outbound traffic to attacker infrastructure named in the article.
ATT&CKT1071
Data sourcesNetwork_Traffic.All_Traffic Web Network_Resolution.DNS DeviceNetworkEvents
DeviceNetworkEvents
| where Timestamp > ago(7d)
| where RemoteIP in ("") or RemoteUrl has_any ("sync.geeker.indevs.in")
| project Timestamp, DeviceName, ActionType, RemoteIP, RemotePort, RemoteUrl,
          InitiatingProcessFileName, InitiatingProcessCommandLine
let BadIPs     = dynamic([""]);
let BadDomains = dynamic(["sync.geeker.indevs.in"]);
ImNetworkSession
| where TimeGenerated > ago(7d)
| where DstIpAddr in (BadIPs)
   or Url has_any (BadDomains)
| project TimeGenerated, DvcHostname, DstIpAddr, DstPortNumber, Url, NetworkProtocol
| order by TimeGenerated desc

DeviceProcessEvents
